STUB TABLES 0x06423C (848 "getters") / 0x064F7C (848 "setters") AND THEIR FIVE DISPATCH SITES -- BLIND TRACE
====================================================================================================
Date            2026-11-27   (blind trace, adversarial review and this deciding repair all on that date;
                what changed and why is in REVIEW HISTORY at the end)
Reference bin   rom/AE5L600L 20g rev 20.19c.bin   md5 92cae8275cd4f9b473a3a9e36efe6449
Method          Every instruction line is printed by scripts/mapping/fn_dump.py (canonical decoder) and
                was read blind (no labels, no docs).  Every contract, class and map line is produced or
                asserted by scripts/trace/emu_stub_tables.py (73460 checks, exit 0) on scripts/sh2e_emu.py.
Arithmetic      Every FPU step (fadd / fsub / fmul / fdiv / fmac / float) is modelled -- in the script and
                in this text -- as the chip does it: exact, then truncated TOWARD ZERO to float32 (FPSCR =
                0x00040001, RM = 01, DN = 1; the ROM's FPU self-test at 0x058696 demands it; corrections
                item 106).  fmac is fused (sh2e_emu.py's rule, not settled by the self-test; nothing here
                depends on it).  This file was first written against an emulator that rounded to nearest;
                re-run under truncation, only the index 0x009 counts moved (REVIEW HISTORY, "RZ RE-RUN").
Verify          python3 scripts/verify_disasm_v2.py --rom "rom/AE5L600L 20g rev 20.19c.bin" <this file>
                The --rom form is the one that counts.  Without it the verifier reads rom/ae5l600l.bin
                (stock) and SKIPS every line at 0x09A3C2..0x09A472 -- the patched words.
EVIDENCE CLASS  bytes + emulated, for the two tables, all 224 stubs, the helpers, the dispatch functions and
                the transport helpers under the two handlers.  Anything that is NOT (protocol names, what
                a RAM variable physically is, 0x00DCE4, what ftrc returns on silicon for NaN / overflow) is
                marked "inferred" / "NOT TRACED" / "not established" where used.

Notation        u8/s8/u16/s16/u32/f32[ADDR] = big-endian read at ADDR.   sat8(x), sat16(x) =
                clamp(trunc(x + 0.5), 0, 255 / 65535) as computed by 0x0BE5A8 / 0x0BE5D8.
                In listings, text after ";" up to " -- " is fn_dump's mechanical annotation; text after
                " -- " is this trace.  A "T = (...)" note states when the compare sets T.

RESULT IN ONE SCREEN
--------------------
 1. There is no 1696-entry table.  0x06423C..0x064F7B holds 848 code pointers and 0x064F7C..0x065CBB holds
    another 848.  They are adjacent, so a pointer-run scanner starting at 0x06423C counts 1696.  Every
    bound check in every caller is "< 848" (literal 0x0350 at 0x09A4DA, 0x09A734, 0x05F3CC, 0x05F686) and
    the second base 0x064F7C is loaded from its own pool slots (0x05F800, 0x09A73C).  The two tables are
    PARALLEL: index i selects getter u32[0x06423C + 4i] and setter u32[0x064F7C + 4i].
 2. THE INDEX is a 24-bit big-endian number taken from a diagnostic request, valid for 0 <= index < 848:
      * handler 0x09A3AC, registered for service id 0xA8 in record table 0x09B778: one index per 3-byte
        item; each returns ONE byte (dispatch at 0x09A466, inside the 175 bytes that differ from stock);
      * handler 0x09A628, registered for service id 0xB8 in 0x09B778 and 0x09B88C: one index + one value
        byte; setter at 0x09A690, then getter at 0x09A69A as a read-back;
      * a second, separate request path (parser 0x05F08C, executor 0x05F316, state block at 0xFFFF8F28)
        that compares its service byte u8[0xFFFF8F41] with the same values 0xA8 / 0xB8 and reaches the
        tables through 0x05F718 (getter, site 0x05F738) and 0x05F758 (setter, site 0x05F78E).
 3. GETTER contract: result in r0, callers keep r0 & 0xFF.  r4 = index at the sites 0x05F738 and 0x09A466,
    but r4 = 0 at the read-back site 0x09A69A (0x09A69C mov #0,r4).  2 of the 191 getter stubs read r4.
    SETTER contract: r4 = value byte, no result used.
 4. 224 distinct stubs (191 getters, 33 setters), 2357 instructions, fall into 63 instruction-pattern
    classes.  486 getter indices share one stub that returns ROM byte 0xFF, 17 share one that returns
    ROM byte 0x00, 816 setter indices share the null stub 0x05E76A (rts / nop).
 5. 288 getter indices read RAM (334 distinct RAM addresses), the other 560 return a constant; the full
    index -> address/formula map is the ";"-prefixed block near the end of this file.
 6. WRITES: through service 0xB8 at 0x09A628 exactly ONE index can be written: 0x060 (whitelist ROM
    0x09CA0C, one entry), and the value must read back.  The 0x05F08C path checks only index < 848.
 7. What the tables ARE, as far as bytes go: a per-address BYTE read/write interface for the low 848
    addresses of the diagnostic services 0xA8 / 0xB8.  That those services are "SSM read/write address"
    and what each address means is protocol knowledge the ROM does not contain -- see NOT ESTABLISHED.
 8. The FF / F2 / F4 path of 0x09A3AC (inside the 175 patched bytes) is not confined to RAM and not bounded:
    it reads 1 / 2 / 4 bytes at 0xFFFF0000 | (b1 << 8) | b2 for ANY b1, b2 -- RAM, the gap above it and the
    on-chip register space alike -- and it stores reply bytes upward from 0xFFFFB3F4 with no limit.  More
    than 88 data bytes overwrite u16[0xFFFFB44C], more than 92 the 0x09CAEA mode byte u8[0xFFFFB450], more
    than 96 the header of the transport ring at 0xFFFFB454 (23 / 24 / 25 F4 items; the length rule admits
    84 items = 336 bytes, to 0xFFFFB543).  The stock handler stores one byte per item, 84 at most.

TABLE GEOMETRY (bytes)
----------------------
;   0x064238: 00 FF 00 00            bytes returned by the two "constant" stubs (0x064238 -> 0x00, 0x064239 -> 0xFF)
;   0x06423C: 848 longs              getter pointers, all inside 0x05D1CA..0x05E75E
;   0x064F7C: 848 longs              setter pointers, all inside 0x05E764..0x05E8D0
;   0x065CBC: 566 bytes              byte map used by getters 0x05DCAE / 0x05DCCE: k = u8[0x065CBC + index - 0x8E]
;   0x065EF4: (next object, a different pointer table used at 0x05F37C -- NOT part of this trace)
;   ROM words equal to 0x0006423C: 0x05F7EC, 0x09A4E4, 0x09A744        (all three are pool slots)
;   ROM words equal to 0x00064F7C: 0x05F800, 0x09A73C                  (both pool slots)
;   The census lists dispatch sites 0x05F738, 0x09A69A (getter) and 0x05F78E, 0x09A690 (setter).  It MISSES
;   the fifth, 0x09A466, because 0x09A45C..0x09A46F is mis-read as literal pool by the function splitter.
;   [Census as it stood when this was written.  The census regenerated later on 2026-11-27 lists all five
;   (summary.dispatch_tables has 0x09A466) and gives 0x09A3AC 151 instructions; it still counts 0x06423C
;   as one table of 1696 -- see A1.]

####################################################################################################
# PART 1 -- WHERE THE INDEX COMES FROM: THE DISPATCH FUNCTIONS
####################################################################################################

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09CAEA .. 0x09CB88  (58 instructions, 1 caller) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = service id (low byte used)
       r5 = 16-bit value handed to the handler as its r4 (the two handlers below use it as request length)
       r6 = selector, low byte: 0 -> record table 0x09B88C, non-zero -> record table 0x09B778
       u8[0xFFFFB450] = shift count for a one-hot "mode" mask (1 << count, 0 when count >= 32)
  out  r0 = 0  a handler was called
       r0 = 1  id not in the selected table
       r0 = 2  id found but (1 << u8[0xFFFFB450]) & record.mask == 0 (handler NOT called)
  side effects: those of the handler.

MECHANISM
  mask = 1 << u8[0xFFFFB450]                 (0x003BF4: r0 << r1 for 0 <= r1 <= 31, 0 for r1 >= 32; emulated
                                              for every count, listing not included -- it ends in a
                                              computed jmp into a ladder of `shll r0`)
  rec  = (r6 & 0xFF) ? 0x09B778 : 0x09B88C
  for n in 0..254:
      id = u8[rec]
      if id == 0xFF: break
      if id == (r4 & 0xFF):
          if (mask & u32[rec + 8]) == 0: return 2
          call u32[rec + 4](r4 = r5_in, r5 = r6_in); return 0
      rec += 12
  return 1
  Record = { u8 id, 3 pad bytes, u32 handler, u32 mask }, 12 bytes.
  Every mask in both tables is 0x00000001, so ANY non-zero u8[0xFFFFB450] gives "return 2" for every id that
  is in the selected table (and still 1 for an id that is not).

EVIDENCE
  table choice 0x09CB0A..0x09CB10 / 0x09CB40; id compare 0x09CB4E; mask test 0x09CB54; handler call 0x09CB5C
  with r4 from the stack word written at 0x09CAFA; terminator 0x09CB76.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x09CAEA", 6 cases (A8/B8 in both tables, mode byte 0 and 1, unknown id);
  mode byte swept 0..255 x (A8, B8 in 0x09B778; B8 in 0x09B88C; unknown id 0x55) = 1024 runs.

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09A3AC .. 0x09A4DA  (151 instructions, 0 direct callers; reached through record table 0x09B778 id 0xA8) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = request length in bytes (16 bit).  The request is fetched with
       0x09D052(r4 = 0xA8, r5 = 0xFFFFB2F6, r6 = min(len, 253)), which copies that many bytes out of the
       transport ring at 0xFFFFB454 (0x09D052 / 0x09CD50, listed below) -- for ANY length: the call at
       0x09A402 comes before the length test at 0x09A406, so a refused request is consumed all the same.
       Request = 1 byte at 0xFFFFB2F6 (never examined in this ROM) + n items of 3 bytes (b0 b1 b2).
  out  none in registers.  Reply appended to the ring by 0x09CFEE and submitted through 0x09CABE, or the
       negative reply 7F A8 code of 0x098686(0xA8, code, 2).
  RAM  u8[0xFFFFB3F3] = 0xE8, reply data from 0xFFFFB3F4 upward, u16[0xFFFFB2F4] = 1 + number of data bytes.
       NOTHING bounds the reply data: the store pointer r10 (0x09A44E / 0x09A450 and 0x09A46C / 0x09A46E)
       is never compared with anything.

MECHANISM
  if not (4 <= len <= 253 and len % 3 == 1): code = 0x13          (request bytes already consumed)
  else for each item, in order:
      b0 == 0xFF : 1 byte  read at A = 0xFFFF0000 | (b1 << 8) | b2
      b0 == 0xF2 : 2 bytes read at A, A+1            (byte by byte, ascending: mov.b @r1+)
      b0 == 0xF4 : 4 bytes read at A .. A+3
                   A is NOT limited to RAM (0xFFFF0000..0xFFFFBFFF).  b1 and b2 are used as they come, so
                   b1 >= 0xC0 addresses the gap above RAM and the on-chip register space (0xFFFFEC0A, the
                   register 0x05F758 toggles, is readable this way), and an F2 / F4 item at the very top
                   runs on past 0xFFFFFFFF to address 0x00000000.
      otherwise  : index = (sext8(b0) << 16) + (b1 << 8) + b2, compared UNSIGNED:
                   index >= 848 -> code = 0x12 and the remaining items are dropped
                   else reply byte = getter[index](r4 = index) & 0xFF        <-- table 0x06423C
  code == 0 : reply E8 + data   (0x09CFEE(0xA8, 0xFFFFB3F3, length); 0x09CFEE(0xA8, ROM 0x09B770, 8);
                                 0x09CABE(&{0xA8, length, 0, 0}))
  code != 0 : 0x098686(0xA8, code, 2)  -> reply 7F A8 code + the same 8 ROM bytes
  So the table index IS the request's 3-byte address whenever its top byte is not FF / F2 / F4.

  REPLY OVERRUN.  The length rule admits 84 items.  A table item or an FF item stores 1 byte (84 at most,
  ending at 0xFFFFB447), but F2 / F4 items store 2 / 4, up to 336 bytes ending at 0xFFFFB543.  What lies in
  that range, from the bytes of the functions that use it:
      0xFFFFB44C  u16 reply-length word of the 0xB8 handler (written at 0x09A6D2)   -- data byte 88 and up
                                                                                      (counting from 0)
      0xFFFFB450  u8 mode byte of 0x09CAEA: non-zero makes it refuse every listed id  -- data byte 92 and up
      0xFFFFB454  transport ring record { u16 write index, u16 read index, u16 mark, data }, the very ring
                  0x09CFEE appends this reply to; 0x09CCF8 stores at record + 6 + write index with no range
                  check on the index (0x09CD26..0x09CD2C)                             -- data byte 96 and up
  i.e. 23 / 24 / 25 F4 items, or 45 / 47 / 49 F2 items.  The length word u16[0xFFFFB2F4] is not limited to a
  byte either (337 for 84 F4 items).  What happens AFTER those bytes are overwritten was not traced, and
  whether any logger sends such a request is outside the ROM.

  Stock bin (rom/ae5l600l.bin) for comparison, decoded but not listed (its bytes differ): same table, same
  bound 848, dispatch at 0x09A45C, NO FF/F2/F4 memory path, the reply byte stored INDEXED by the item
  number (stock 0x09A466: mov.b r2,@(r0,r6) with r6 = 0xFFFFB3F4, r0 = item number -- so it cannot pass
  0xFFFFB447), and it additionally rejects a non-zero first byte with code 0x12 (mov.b @(2,r12),r0 /
  tst r0,r0 / bf at 0x09A408..0x09A40C of the stock image).

EVIDENCE
  length rule 0x09A3C6..0x09A3E8; fetch 0x09A402 BEFORE the length test 0x09A406..0x09A40E; top-byte
  classification 0x09A432..0x09A442; address 0x09A446..0x09A44A; byte loop 0x09A44C..0x09A456 (no compare
  on r1 or r10); bound 0x09A414 + 0x09A45A; dispatch 0x09A45E..0x09A468; reply 0x09A48A..0x09A4C4.
  fn_dump prints 0x09A45C..0x09A46F and 0x09A474 as "pool" when started at 0x09A3AC; those bytes are code
  (branch target of 0x09A442 and 0x09A45C).  They are listed here from `fn_dump 0x09A45A --end 0x09A476`.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x09A3AC": 5 table indices in one request; addresses 000350 / 010008 /
  7F0008 / 800008 / F30008 -> code 0x12; FF6000 / F26000 / F46000 -> 1 / 2 / 4 bytes of 0xFFFF6000; lengths
  3, 5, 6, 254 -> code 0x13; 0x09D052 called with r6 = min(len, 253) for lengths 0, 3, 5, 7, 253, 254,
  0x1234; first byte 00 / 01 / 02 / 80 / FF -> identical reply.
  "tagged items": FF EC 0A -> one read of 0xFFFFEC0A; F2 EC 0A -> reads of 0xFFFFEC0A, 0xFFFFEC0B;
  FF C0 00 -> the read at 0x09A44C addresses 0xFFFFC000; F4 FF FE -> the bytes of 0xFFFFFFFE, 0xFFFFFFFF,
  0x00000000, 0x00000001; 22 / 23 / 24 / 25 / 84 x F4 -> 88 / 92 / 96 / 100 / 336 bytes stored, with
  0xFFFFB44C / 0xFFFFB450 / 0xFFFFB454 overwritten from 23 / 24 / 25 items on; 85 items refused (0x13);
  84 table items -> 84 bytes.
  "transport": the same request shapes with 0x09D052 / 0x09CFEE / 0x098686 / 0x09CABE run for real (only
  0x00DCE4 stubbed): ring receives E8 + data + 8 x 00, or 7F A8 12 / 7F A8 13 + 8 x 00.

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09A628 .. 0x09A710  (116 instructions, 0 direct callers; reached through record tables 0x09B778 and 0x09B88C, id 0xB8) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = request length (16 bit); request bytes b0 b1 b2 b3 fetched with 0x09D052(0xB8, sp, 4) from the
       transport ring -- only when len == 4: a wrong length branches away at 0x09A63E BEFORE the fetch,
       so (unlike 0x09A3AC) a refused request is left unread in the ring
  out  none in registers; reply through 0x09CFEE / 0x09CABE or 0x098686(0xB8, code, 2)
  RAM  whatever setter[index] writes; u16[0xFFFFB44C] = 2 on success.

MECHANISM
  if len != 4:                              code = 0x13      (request not fetched)
  index = (b0 << 16) + (b1 << 8) + b2       (all zero-extended)
  elif index >= 848 (unsigned):             code = 0x22
  elif 0x09A710(index) != 0:                code = 0x22      (index not in the ROM whitelist {0x60})
  elif u32[0x064F7C + 4*index] == 0x05E76A: code = 0x22      (null setter)
  else:
      setter[index](r4 = b3)                                 <-- table 0x064F7C
      rb = getter[index](r4 = 0) & 0xFF                      <-- table 0x06423C, r4 is 0 here, NOT the index
      code = 0 if rb == b3 else 0x22
  code == 0 : reply F8 b3  (0x09CFEE(0xB8, sp+8, 2); 0x09CFEE(0xB8, ROM 0x09B770, 8); 0x09CABE(&{0xB8, 2, 0, 0}))
  code != 0 : 0x098686(0xB8, code, 2)
  Net effect in this ROM: only index 0x060 passes; its setter 0x05E76E writes u8[0xFFFF36BE] = b3 and its
  getter 0x05D7A4 returns u8[0xFFFF36BE], so every value reads back and the reply is always F8 b3.
  The r4 = 0 read-back would be wrong for getters 0x05DCAE / 0x05DCCE (the only ones that use r4), but no
  index served by them is whitelisted.

EVIDENCE
  length 0x09A638; index 0x09A64C..0x09A65E; bound 0x09A660..0x09A664; whitelist 0x09A668..0x09A670;
  null-setter test 0x09A678..0x09A682; setter 0x09A68C..0x09A692; getter 0x09A694..0x09A69C;
  compare 0x09A69E..0x09A6AA.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x09A628": 9 request cases + forced read-back mismatch + argument capture
  (setter r4 = value, getter r4 = 0).  "transport": 7 of the cases again with 0x09D052 / 0x09CFEE /
  0x098686 / 0x09CABE run for real: ring receives F8 v + 8 x 00 or 7F B8 code + 8 x 00; the read index
  moves by 4 when len == 4 and by 0 otherwise.

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09A710 .. 0x09A734  (18 instructions, 1 caller) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = index
  out  r0 = 0 if index == u32[0x09CA0C] (= 0x00000060), else 1
  no side effects.

MECHANISM
  for i in 0 .. N-1 with N = 1 (immediate at 0x09A712): if u32[0x09CA0C + 4i] == r4: return 0
  return 1
  The same register holds N and the "not found" result, so the routine only works as written for N = 1.

EVIDENCE
  0x09A712 (N), 0x09A71A (list), 0x09A720 (compare), 0x09A72C (loop bound).  ROM 0x09CA0C: 00 00 00 60.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x09A710": 0x60 -> 0; 0, 0x5F, 0x61, 0x34F, 0x6000, 0x10060 -> 1.

; ---- TRANSPORT HELPERS UNDER THE TWO HANDLERS -------------------------------------------------------
; The first version of this file stubbed these and marked them "NOT TRACED / assumed".  They are short and
; run in the emulator.  Common object, from the literals 0xFFFFB454 / 608 / 601 / 600 in 0x09CD50, 0x09CCF8
; and 0x09CC44:
;   record(n) = 0xFFFFB454 + 608 * n :  +0 u16 write index   +2 u16 read index   +4 u16 mark
;                                       +6 601 data bytes (indices 0..600, wrapping to 0)
;   u8[0xFFFFB6DF + 2n] = the id byte channel n is bound to.  The three wrappers below scan n < 1 only
;   (immediate 1 at 0x09D052 / 0x09CFEE / 0x09D086), so only record(0) = 0xFFFFB454 is reachable through them.
;   Unread data = data[read index .. mark]; the reader is "empty" when read index == mark + 1, or when
;   mark >= 600 and read index == 0.  The writer drops a byte when write index == mark.
; These six functions and 0x098686 / 0x09CABE are byte-identical in rom/ae5l600l.bin (stock).

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09D052 .. 0x09D084  (25 instructions, 13 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = id (low byte), r5 = destination, r6 = byte count (passed through untouched)
  out  if u8[0xFFFFB6DF] == id: 0x09CD50(0, r5, r6) -- count bytes copied out of record(0) at the read index
       else nothing: the destination is left as it was

MECHANISM
  for n in 0 .. 0:  if u8[0xFFFFB6DF + 2n] == (r4 & 0xFF): 0x09CD50(n, r5, r6); break
  That the unread bytes of the ring ARE the request that follows the id byte is set upstream (0x09CA18 and
  whatever fills the ring) -- not traced.  What is established is the copy and its id gate.

EVIDENCE
  0x09D058 + 0x09D062..0x09D06C (gate), 0x09D06E..0x09D070 (call), 0x09D052 + 0x09D078 (bound 1).
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "transport": channel bound to 0xA8 / asked 0xA8 -> 4 bytes copied; bound
  0xA8 / asked 0xB8 and bound 0x00 / asked 0xA8 -> destination untouched; and through both handlers.

LISTING

09D054: 2FE6  mov.l r14,@-r15
09D056: EE00  mov #0,r14                    ; n = 0
09D058: D039  mov.l @(0x09D140),r0          ; = 0xFFFFB6DF RAM -- r0 -> id-binding bytes u8[FFFFB6DF + 2n]

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09CD50 .. 0x09CDD4  (66 instructions, 4 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = channel n (low byte), r5 = destination, r6 = byte count (low 16 bits)
  out  count bytes at destination; u16[record + 2] (read index) advanced.  r0, r2..r7 clobbered, MACL preserved.

MECHANISM
  rec = 0xFFFFB454 + 608 * n
  for i in 0 .. count-1:
      mark = u16[rec + 4];  rd = u16[rec + 2]
      if rd == mark + 1 or (mark >= 600 and rd == 0):   dst[i] = 0              (nothing unread)
      else:  dst[i] = u8[rec + 6 + rd];  rd += 1;  if rd >= 601: rd = 0;  u16[rec + 2] = rd
  The destination always receives count bytes; missing ones are 0.

EVIDENCE
  record address 0x09CD54..0x09CD7C; empty test 0x09CD7E..0x09CD9A; copy 0x09CD9C..0x09CDA6; index
  advance and wrap 0x09CDA8..0x09CDB8; loop 0x09CDBA..0x09CDC2.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "transport": 9 (write, read, mark, count) states x channel 0 / 1 against
  a Python model of the above (wrap at 600 -> 0, running dry mid-copy, both "empty" forms).

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x09CCF8 .. 0x09CD50  (44 instructions, 4 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = channel n (low byte), r5 = source, r6 = byte count (low 16 bits)
  out  bytes stored in the record's data area; u16[record + 0] (write index) advanced.  MACL preserved.

MECHANISM
  rec = 0xFFFFB454 + 608 * n
  for each of count source bytes:
      if u16[rec + 4] == u16[rec + 0]:  the byte is dropped              (write index has reached mark)
      else:  u8[rec + 6 + u16[rec + 0]] = byte;  wr += 1;  if wr >= 601: wr = 0;  u16[rec + 0] = wr
  The store address is rec + 6 + (zero-extended write index) with NO check that the index is below 601
  (0x09CD26..0x09CD2C): the wrap test comes after the store.  A write index that something else has
  overwritten (see REPLY OVERRUN under 0x09A3AC) therefore redirects the store.

EVIDENCE
  record address 0x09CCFC..0x09CD16; full test 0x09CD18..0x09CD1E; store 0x09CD20..0x09CD2C; index advance
  and wrap 0x09CD2E..0x09CD3C; loop 0x09CD3E..0x09CD44.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "transport": 9 states x channel 0 / 1 against a Python model (wrap,
  stopping at mark).

LISTING

09CCFC: 936A  mov.w @(0x09CDD4),r3          ; = 0x0260 (608) -- r3 = 608
09CCFE: 646D  extu.w r6,r4                  ; r4 = count (16 bit)

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x098686 .. 0x098728  (63 instructions, 14 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 = id, r5 = code, r6 = kind (both handlers above pass 2)
  out  kind == 2: write index reset to mark + 1 (0x09D084), then the bytes 7F, id, code and the 8 bytes of
                  ROM 0x09B770 (all 00) are appended (four 0x09CFEE calls); 0x09CABE(&{id, 3, 8, 0})
       kind == 1: nothing appended; 0x09CABE(&{id, 0, 8, 1})                       (bytes only, not emulated)
       otherwise: nothing

MECHANISM
  see listing.  The reply to a refused 0xA8 / 0xB8 request is therefore 7F A8 code / 7F B8 code, and
  anything a handler had already appended is discarded first.

EVIDENCE
  kind test 0x098692..0x098696 and 0x098700..0x098702; reset 0x09869E; the three single bytes 0x0986A2..
  0x0986C0; ROM tail 0x0986C4..0x0986CA; struct 0x0986CC..0x0986DA + 0x098712; submit 0x098718.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "transport": through 0x09A3AC (codes 0x12, 0x13) and 0x09A628 (0x13, 0x22):
  ring receives 7F id code + 8 x 00 and 0x00DCE4 is entered with r6 -> {id, 3, 8, 0}.

==== FUNCTION 0x09CABE .. 0x09CACC  (7 instructions, 10 callers) ====

CONTRACT
  in   r4 = pointer to four longs {id, length, x, y}
  out  tail jump 0x00DCE4(r4 = 2, r5 = 1, r6 = that pointer)

MECHANISM
  0x00DCE4 is NOT TRACED (it indexes a table at 0x011B98 and makes an unresolved indirect call).  What the
  third long means (0 after a positive reply, 8 after the negative reply of 0x098686) is not established.

EVIDENCE
  bytes; emulated with 0x00DCE4 stubbed and its arguments captured: (2, 1, &{0xA8, length, 0, 0}),
  (2, 1, &{0xB8, 2, 0, 0}), (2, 1, &{id, 3, 8, 0}).

==== FUNCTION 0x05F08C .. 0x05F2FC  (283 instructions, 2 callers) -- PARTIAL: 158 instructions in 5 fragments ====

CONTRACT
  (only what the index needs)
  in   GBR is set to 0xFFFF8F34 at 0x05F09A.  u8[0xFFFF8F41] = service byte; u8[0xFFFF8F40] = a count used by
       the 0xA8 branch; u8[0xFFFF8F42..] = request data.
  out  flags word u16[0xFFFF9048]: bits 0, 3, 8, 9, 10, 11, 12 are cleared, then one request flag is set.
       Bit 15: on the 0xA8 branch it is a copy of bit 0 of u8[0xFFFF8F42] (set by 0x05F2FC and kept); on the
       0xB8 branch and on the refusal path it is cleared.
       u8[0xFFFF8F36] = reply id, u8[0xFFFF8F3A] = count, u8[0xFFFF8F34] = count + 4.

MECHANISM
  service = u8[0xFFFF8F41]
  0xA0 -> 0x05F126     0xA8 -> 0x05F1B4     0xB0 -> 0x05F1EC     0xB8 -> 0x05F25A
  0xBF -> 0x05F29A     0x9F -> 0x05F2B0     anything else -> 0x05F2CA (flags |= 0x0001, bit 15 cleared)
  0xA8:  0x05F2FC(): flags bit 15 := bit 0 of u8[0xFFFF8F42]   (the byte in front of the 3-byte items)
         u8[0xFFFF8F3A] = (u8[0xFFFF8F40] - 2)/3 + 1;  u8[0xFFFF8F36] = 0xE8;  flags |= 0x1000
         (stored at 0x05F2E4, which is past the "clear bit 15" of 0x05F2E0..0x05F2E2: bit 15 survives)
  0xB8:  index = (u8[0xFFFF8F42] << 16) + (u8[0xFFFF8F43] << 8) + u8[0xFFFF8F44]
         if index >= 848 (unsigned): goto 0x05F2CA (flags |= 0x0001)
         u8[0xFFFF8F3A] = 2;  u8[0xFFFF8F36] = 0xF8;  flags |= 0x0400;  bit 15 cleared
  The 0xA0 / 0xB0 / 0xBF / 0x9F branches were NOT analysed (0xA0 also starts with a call of 0x05F2FC, at
  0x05F126).  The 0xB8 branch has no whitelist and no null-setter test: the bound is its only check.

EVIDENCE

  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x05F08C": service 0xA8 with u8[0xFFFF8F42] swept 0..255 x 3 (count,
  incoming flags) cases -> flags == (incoming, bits 0/3/8..12 cleared) with bit 15 = bit 0 of that byte and

CONTRACT
  in   u8[0xFFFF8F42]; flags word u16[0xFFFF9048]
  out  u16[0xFFFF9048] bit 15 = bit 0 of u8[0xFFFF8F42]; the other 15 bits unchanged.  r0, r2, r5, r6 clobbered.

MECHANISM
  if u8[0xFFFF8F42] & 1: flags |= 0x8000   else: flags &= 0x7FFF
  u8[0xFFFF8F42] is the byte in front of the 3-byte items of a 0xA8 request; the executor 0x05F316 tests
  bit 1 of the same byte (0x05F5B6..0x05F5BA).  What reads bit 15 afterwards was not traced.

EVIDENCE
  0x05F2FC..0x05F2FE (base 0xFFFF8F3D + 5), 0x05F300..0x05F304, 0x05F308..0x05F310, store 0x05F314.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py E "05F2FC": every byte value 0..255 x 5 flag words, and through 0x05F08C
  (F "0x05F08C").

LISTING

05F2FE: 8465  mov.b @(5,r6),r0              ; r0 = s8[FFFF8F3D + 5] = the byte at 0xFFFF8F42
05F300: C801  tst #1,r0                     ; T = (bit 0 == 0)
05F302: D655  mov.l @(0x05F458),r6          ; = 0xFFFF9048 RAM -- r6 -> flags word
05F304: 8D03  bt/s 0x05F30E                 ; taken when T=1 (delay slot runs first) -- bit 0 clear -> 0x05F30E (clear bit 15)

==== FUNCTION 0x05F316 .. 0x05F62A  (373 instructions, 1 caller) -- PARTIAL: 197 instructions in 3 fragments ====

CONTRACT
  in   flags word f = u16[0xFFFF9048] (set by 0x05F08C); request bytes at 0xFFFF8F42..; u8[0xFFFF8F38],
       u8[0xFFFF8F3A] (running item number and count+1 for the 0x1000 path).
  out  bytes in the 4-byte buffer 0xFFFF903C[pos], pos = u8[0xFFFF8F3B].
  (fragments 0x05F372..0x05F4BC = flag bits 8, 9, 3 and 0x05F4EC..0x05F528 = bit 11 were NOT analysed)

MECHANISM
  if f & 0x0001: return                       elif f & 0x0100: goto 0x05F372
  elif f & 0x0200: goto 0x05F43C              elif f & 0x0400: goto 0x05F4BE
  elif f & 0x0800: goto 0x05F4EC              elif f & 0x1000: goto 0x05F52A
  elif f & 0x0008: goto 0x05F3D2              else return

  0x05F4BE (flag 0x0400 = service 0xB8):
      u16[0xFFFF8F28] = (u8[0xFFFF8F43] << 8) + u8[0xFFFF8F44]        index word (low 16 bits of the index)
      u8[0xFFFF8F3C]  = u8[0xFFFF8F45]                                 value byte
      f |= 0x0040;  0x05F758()   -> setter[index](value)
      0x05F718()                 -> out[pos] = getter[index](r4 = index) & 0xFF; clears bit 6
      No bound check HERE: it relies on 0x05F27E in the parser.  No whitelist, no null-setter test, and the
      read-back is returned, not compared.

  0x05F52A (flag 0x1000 = service 0xA8), at most 4 items per call (pos 0..3):
      while pos <= 3 and u8[0xFFFF8F3A] - 1 > item:
          addr = (b0 << 16) + (b1 << 8) + b2  with b0,b1,b2 = u8[0xFFFF8F43 + 3*item ..+2]
          if (u8[0xFFFF8F42] & 0x02) == 0 and addr < 848:
              u16[0xFFFF8F28] = (b1 << 8) + b2;  f |= 0x0040               -> table path
          else:
              u32[0xFFFF9044] = addr + (0xFF000000 if b0 & 0xF0 else 0);  0x05F62A()   -> memory path
          0x05F718();  item += 1;  pos += 1
      pos = 0
  So on this path too the table index is the request's 3-byte address, and bit 1 of the byte before the
  items (u8[0xFFFF8F42]) forces the memory path even for addresses below 848.

EVIDENCE
  flag chain 0x05F316..0x05F36A; index word 0x05F4D6 and 0x05F5E8; bound 0x05F54A + 0x05F5BC; bit-1 bypass
  0x05F5B6..0x05F5BA; calls 0x05F4E0 / 0x05F4E8 / 0x05F5FA.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x05F316": flag 0x0400 with indices 0x060, 0x000, 0x083, 0x008, 0x34F;
  flag 0x1000 with items 000008, 00000E, FF6000, 00034F (table, table, RAM byte, table) and with bit 1 set.

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x05F718 .. 0x05F758  (32 instructions, 1 caller: 0x05F316, at 0x05F492 / 0x05F504 / 0x05F5FA and tail-reached from 0x05F4E8) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   f = u16[0xFFFF9048]; index word u16[0xFFFF8F28]; memory pointer u32[0xFFFF9044]; pos = u8[0xFFFF8F3B]
  out  u8[0xFFFF903C + pos] = result byte;  bit 6 of f is cleared when it was set
  r0..r6 clobbered; nothing returned.

MECHANISM
  if f & 0x40:   f &= ~0x40;  result = getter[u16 index](r4 = index)        <-- table 0x06423C, site 0x05F738
  elif f & 0x10: result = u8[u32[0xFFFF9044]]
  else:          result = 0xFF
  u8[0xFFFF903C + pos] = result & 0xFF
  The index is used as a full 16-bit word with NO bound check here; both writers of bit 6 (0x05F4DE after
  the parser bound, 0x05F5EC after 0x05F5BC) have index < 848.

EVIDENCE
  0x05F71C..0x05F720 (bit 6), 0x05F72A..0x05F73A (dispatch), 0x05F740..0x05F748 (bit 4 / default 0xFF),
  0x05F74A..0x05F756 (store).
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x05F718": 16 indices x 2 (pos, flags) pairs against a direct call of the
  same getter; flags 0x0010 -> memory byte; 0x0000 / 0x0020 / 0x0080 -> 0xFF; flags untouched when bit 6 clear.

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x05F758 .. 0x05F7BE  (51 instructions, 1 caller: 0x05F316, at 0x05F48E / 0x05F4E0) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   f = u16[0xFFFF9048]; index word u16[0xFFFF8F28]; value byte u8[0xFFFF8F3C]; pointer u32[0xFFFF9044]
  out  RAM written by the setter, or u8[pointer] = value
  side effects: SR saved / interrupt mask raised to 0xE0 around the write (0x0BE81C / 0x0BE82C); bit 0 of the
  on-chip word register 0xFFFFEC0A is set first when f bit 7 is set, and always cleared afterwards.
  f itself is NOT modified (bit 6 is cleared later by 0x05F718).

MECHANISM
  0x0BE81C(&saved, 0xE0)
  if f & 0x80: u16[0xFFFFEC0A] |= 1
  if f & 0x40:   setter[u16 index](r4 = sext8(value))                       <-- table 0x064F7C, site 0x05F78E
  elif f & 0x20: u8[u32[0xFFFF9044]] = value
  u16[0xFFFFEC0A] &= ~1
  0x0BE82C(saved)

EVIDENCE
  0x05F764..0x05F76C, 0x05F770..0x05F77A, 0x05F77C..0x05F790, 0x05F796..0x05F7A4, 0x05F7A6..0x05F7B2.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py F "0x05F758" with 0x0BE81C / 0x0BE82C stubbed (the emulator refuses ldc sr)
  and 0xFFFFEC0A as a logged scratch register: 7 indices x flags 0x0040 / 0x00C0; flags 0x0020 (memory
  write), 0x0000 and 0x0010 (nothing written).

LISTING

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x0BE81C .. 0x0BE82C  (8 instructions, 192 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in r4 = pointer, r5 = new SR value (callers here pass 0xE0)
  out u32[r4] = SR & 0xF0;  SR = r5 if r5 >= (SR & 0xF0) (unsigned) else SR & 0xF0

MECHANISM
  old = SR & 0xF0; u32[r4] = old; SR = max(r5, old)      (every SR bit outside the loaded value becomes 0)

EVIDENCE
  bytes only: `ldc r5,sr` is not emulated (stubbed in emu_stub_tables.py).

LISTING

0BE822: 8D01  bt/s 0x0BE828                 ; taken when T=1 (delay slot runs first) -- delay slot: [r4] = SR & 0xF0
0BE824: 2402  mov.l r0,@r4
0BE826: 6503  mov r0,r5                     ; requested mask lower than current -> keep current
0BE828: 000B  rts
0BE82A: 450E  ldc r5,sr                     ; delay slot: SR = r5 (all other SR bits become 0)

# PART 2 -- HELPERS AND SMALL CALLEES USED BY THE STUBS

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x0BE5A8 .. 0x0BE5D0  (20 instructions, 105 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in fr4 = x, fr5 = scale, fr6 = offset
  out r0 = clamp(trunc((x - offset) / scale + 0.5), 0, 255)
  clobbers r4, r5, fr2, fr3, fr4

MECHANISM
  v = (fr4 - fr6) / fr5;  r = ftrc(v + 0.5);  if r > 255: r = 255;  elif r < 0: r = 0;  return r
  Divide, not multiply: a stub that passes fr5 = 0.25 returns 4 counts per unit.

EVIDENCE
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py E, 1015 (x, scale, offset) cases, exact match including the .5 rounding edges,
  against a model in which the fsub, the fdiv and the fadd 0.5 each truncate TOWARD ZERO (FPSCR RM = 01,
  corrections item 106).  Consequence: for 0 <= y = (x - offset) / scale < 2^23, "+ 0.5, ftrc" is exactly
  floor(y + 0.5) -- an exact sum just below an integer is never rounded up to it (round-to-nearest would
  be).  The 0.5 boundaries are therefore exact in y; what can move them is the fsub / fdiv before it.

LISTING
0BE5A8: C70A  mova @(0x0BE5D4),r0           ; -> 0x0BE5D4: long 0x3F000000 f32 0.5 -- r0 -> 0.5
0BE5AA: F461  fsub fr6,fr4                  ; fr4 = x - off
0BE5AC: 9510  mov.w @(0x0BE5D0),r5          ; = 0x00FF (255) -- r5 = 255
0BE5AE: F453  fdiv fr5,fr4                  ; fr4 = (x - off) / scale
0BE5B0: F308  fmov.s @r0,fr3
0BE5B2: F24C  fmov fr4,fr2
0BE5B4: F230  fadd fr3,fr2                  ; fr2 = fr4 + 0.5
0BE5B6: F23D  ftrc fr2,fpul                 ; truncate toward zero
0BE5B8: 045A  sts fpul,r4
0BE5BA: 3457  cmp/gt r5,r4                  ; T = (r4 > 255) signed
0BE5BC: 8F02  bf/s 0x0BE5C4                 ; taken when T=0 (delay slot runs first) -- taken when r4 <= 255
0BE5BE: 0009  nop
0BE5C0: A004  bra 0x0BE5CC
0BE5C2: 6453  mov r5,r4                     ; delay slot: r4 = 255
0BE5C4: 4411  cmp/pz r4                     ; T = (r4 >= 0)
0BE5C6: 8D01  bt/s 0x0BE5CC                 ; taken when T=1 (delay slot runs first)
0BE5C8: 0009  nop
0BE5CA: E400  mov #0,r4                     ; negative -> 0
0BE5CC: 000B  rts
0BE5CE: 6043  mov r4,r0
;   pool 0x0BE5D0: .long 0x00FF0000  const

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x0BE598 .. 0x0BE5A6  (7 instructions, 98 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in r4 = raw (low 16 bits used), fr4 = k, fr5 = c
  out fr0 = c + k * float(raw & 0xFFFF)

MECHANISM
  fmac fr0,fr3,fr5 : fr5 += fr0 * fr3

EVIDENCE
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py E, 40 cases, against c + k * float(raw) computed exactly and truncated toward
  zero ONCE (fused fmac -- scripts/sh2e_emu.py's rule; the ROM's FPU self-test does not settle fused vs
  unfused).  7 of the 80 cases of the two helpers (k = 0.005, c = -1) would differ if fmac were unfused.
  No stub depends on it: every stub that calls 0x0BE598 / 0x0BE588 passes c = 0 (fldi0 fr5), asserted
  per getter formula, so the result is k * float(raw) truncated once either way.

LISTING
0BE598: 644D  extu.w r4,r4                  ; r4 = raw & 0xFFFF
0BE59A: 445A  lds r4,fpul
0BE59C: F32D  float fpul,fr3                ; fr3 = float(raw)
0BE59E: F04C  fmov fr4,fr0                  ; fr0 = k
0BE5A0: F53E  fmac fr0,fr3,fr5              ; fr5 = fr5 + fr0*fr3 = c + k*raw
0BE5A2: 000B  rts
0BE5A4: F05C  fmov fr5,fr0                  ; delay slot: fr0 = result

==== FUNCTION 0x0BE980 .. 0x0BE98E  (7 instructions, 174 callers) ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in r4 = pointer to a 16-bit word
  out r0 = 0 exactly when u8[r4+1] == ~u8[r4] (the pair 0x0BE9A0 builds); otherwise non-zero (0..255)

MECHANISM
  r0 = (~word - (word >> 8)) & 0xFF

EVIDENCE
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py SPECIAL, 264 words.

0BE98A: 000B  rts
0BE98C: 600C  extu.b r0,r0                  ; delay slot: r0 &= 0xFF -> 0 exactly when low byte == ~high byte

####################################################################################################
# PART 3 -- ALL 224 TABLE TARGETS, GROUPED MECHANICALLY BY INSTRUCTION PATTERN
####################################################################################################

Method: every distinct target is decoded to its end (reachability sweep; the 224 extents equal the census
instruction counts); the signature is the instruction text with PC-relative literals replaced by their
address class, immediates and displacements masked, branch targets made relative, and jsr/jmp replaced by
the RESOLVED callee.  Identical signatures form a class.  G = reached from table 0x06423C, S = from 0x064F7C;
no stub is in both tables.  "idx" = number of table indices served.

; class  stubs   idx  insns  example   what the class does
; G00      10   511     3   05D1CA    returns a ROM byte: r0 = s8[ROM address]
; G01      37    37     3   05D1DA    returns a RAM byte: r0 = s8[RAM address] (callers keep the low byte)
; G02       4     4    11   05D204    sat8(f32[RAM] - off): scale 1.0 (fldi1), offset from the pool
; G03       1     1    13   05D2C0    sat8(((f32[RAM] + -1) - -1) / k): fr6 = -1 is added to the value AND passed as the offset.  x + -1 is truncated toward zero, the helper's subtraction is then exact: equal to sat8(f32[RAM] / k) except for 576 float32 inputs, all in (0, 0.5) and less than 2^-24 (6e-8) below a rounding boundary, where it is one count higher
; G04       2     2    12   05D2DA    sat8((f32[RAM] - -1) / k)
; G05       1     1    11   05D2F2    constant: sat8((-1 - -1) / k) = 0, no RAM input (fr4 := fr6 = -1)
; G06       2     2    11   05D308    constant: sat8((0 - -1) / k), no RAM input
; G07      18    18    11   05D31E    sat8(f32[RAM] / k), offset 0
; G08       4     4    16   05D334    HIGH byte of sat16(f32[RAM] / k)
; G09       4     4    11   05D354    LOW byte of sat16(f32[RAM] / k) (returns all 16 bits; callers keep the low byte)
; G10       3     3    10   05D36A    sat8(f32[RAM]): scale 1, offset 0
; G11       6     6    12   05D37E    sat8((f32[RAM] - off) / k), offset and scale from the pool
; G12      16    16     2   05D3F8    constant 0 (rts / mov #0,r0)
; G13       5     5    18   05D454    sat8((k1 * float(u16[RAM + disp])) / k2): raw word -> float via 0x0BE598, then 0x0BE5A8
; G14       1     1    12   05D48E    sat8(f32[RAM base + immediate index] / k)
; G15       1     1    15   05D4BC    sat8(((f32[B] - f32[A]) - off) / k): difference of two RAM floats
; G16       1     1    15   05D4DA    sat8(((f32[A] + f32[B]) - off) / k): sum of two RAM floats
; G17       6     6    10   05D50E    constant: sat8(0 - off), no RAM input
; G18       4     4     9   05D538    constant: sat8(0) = 0, no RAM input
; G19       1     1    14   05D560    sat8((f32[RAM] * m) / k)
; G20       1     1    20   05D640    as G13 with the word loaded by mov.w @(r0,r6)
; G21       3     3    11   05D73E    constant: sat8((0 - off) / k), no RAM input
; G22      10    10    10   05D790    constant: sat8(0 / k) = 0, no RAM input
; G23       1     1     9   05D7A4    HIGH byte of u16[RAM] (= u8[RAM])
; G24       1     1    64   05D7B6    status byte: each output bit is a test of one RAM byte (two of them through 0x058504 / 0x022CEA)
; G25       1     1    80   05D8A8    status byte: 7 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G26       1     1    42   05D948    status byte: 4 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G27       1     1    70   05D99C    status byte: bits 7,6 copied from u8[FFFF8F17], 5 bits each (RAM byte == 1)
; G28       1     1    48   05DA28    status byte: 4 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G29       1     1    51   05DA88    status byte: (RAM byte == 1), |f32| outside a +-0.390625 band (0x0BE608), (RAM byte == 1) via 0x061496
; G30       6     6     5   05DAEE    constant 0 written through a stack temporary
; G31       1     1    75   05DB60    status byte: 5 bits (RAM byte == 1), 2 bits (RAM byte == 0)
; G32       1     1    15   05DBF6    one bit: set when the RAM byte == 0
; G33       4     4    15   05DC14    one bit: set when the RAM byte == 1
; G34       1     1    10   05DC6E    sat8(f32[RAM]) (as G10, different instruction order)
; G35      13    13     4   05DC94    returns a RAM byte, zero-extended
; G36       1    79    13   05DCAE    u8[0xFFFFAD52 + k], k = ROM u8[0x065C2E + r4]: USES r4 (= the index at sites 0x05F738 / 0x09A466; 0x09A69A passes 0)
; G37       1    79    13   05DCCE    u8[0xFFFF3B06 + 2k], k = ROM u8[0x065C2E + r4]: USES r4 (= the index at sites 0x05F738 / 0x09A466; 0x09A69A passes 0)
; G38       1     1    15   05DD34    one bit: set when the RAM byte != 0
; G39       1     1    86   05DDF4    status byte: 8 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G40       1     1    25   05DEC6    constant 0 (clears six bits of a zeroed stack temporary)
; G41       1     1     6   05DEF8    validated byte: high byte of the word at 0xFFFF8F0C if it is a (byte, ~byte) pair, else 0
; G42       1     1    77   05DF20    u8[0xFFFF83AC] & 0xF3, bit by bit through six one-bit readers
; G43       1     1    18   05E0F4    as G13 with the word loaded by mov.w @r6
; G44       1     1     9   05E1E0    HIGH byte of u16[RAM] (as G23, different registers)
; G45       1     1     3   05E1F2    s16[RAM]; callers keep the low byte = u8[RAM + 1]
; G46       1     1    73   05E2EA    status byte: 6 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G47       1     1    75   05E3E8    status byte: 7 bits, each (RAM byte == 1)
; G48       1     1    55   05E580    status byte: 4 bits, each (RAM byte == 1), one through 0x058524
; G49       1     1     5   05E63C    byte 0 (most significant) of u32[RAM]
; G50       1     1     4   05E646    byte 1 of u32[RAM]
; G51       1     1     4   05E710    byte 2 of u32[RAM]
; G52       1     1     3   05E718    u32[RAM]; callers keep the low byte = byte 3
; S53      10    10     3   05E764    u8[RAM] = r4 (low byte)
; S54       3   818     2   05E76A    nothing (rts / nop)
; S55      10    10     3   05E76E    u8[RAM] = r4 (low byte) (as S53, different register)
; S56       1     1    10   05E774    f32[RAM] = float(r4 & 0xFF) via 0x0BE588
; S57       4     4     8   05E788    u16[RAM] = (v << 8) | (~v & 0xFF) via 0x0BE9A0
; S58       1     1    12   05E7C2    shuffles r4 through stack temporaries; no RAM written
; S59       1     1    20   05E7FA    v -> 0x04E876: u8[FFFF83AC] = v, u16[FFFF341A] = pair(v & 1)
; S60       1     1    20   05E844    bit 1 of v -> 0x02E364; bit 0 of v -> 0x02E34E, else 0x02E35A
; S61       1     1     3   05E8A2    tail-jump to 0x05AA30 with r4 = v & 0xFF
; S62       1     1    14   05E8A8    u8[RAM] = v through stack temporaries

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x05D1CA .. 0x05D1D0  (3 instructions, 3 dispatch sites: 0x05F738, 0x09A466, 0x09A69A) -- CLASS G00 EXAMPLE ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 is not read by this stub (sites 0x05F738 / 0x09A466 pass the index, 0x09A69A passes 0);  no RAM written
  out  r0; the dispatch sites keep r0 & 0xFF
  this stub at index 0x006:  0
  ROM data read: 064238=00

MECHANISM
  class G00: returns a ROM byte: r0 = s8[ROM address]
  10 stub(s) in the class (stub@index):
    05D1CA@17idx 05D1D0@486idx 05D1E0@001 05D1E6@002 05D1EC@003 05D1F2@004 05D1F8@005 05DEAC@0DA 05DEB2@0DB
    05DEB8@0DC
  pattern: mov.l @(POOL:ROM),r2 | rts | mov.b @r2,r0

EVIDENCE
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py C (symbolic): formula derived by symbolic execution of these bytes, then
  compared with the emulator on 12 seeded-random RAM states for every index that uses the class.

LISTING
05D1CA: D231  mov.l @(0x05D290),r2          ; = 0x00064238 ROM
05D1CC: 000B  rts                           ; returns; callers keep r0 & 0xFF = 0
05D1CE: 6020  mov.b @r2,r0
;   pool 0x05D290: .long 0x00064238  ROM

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x05D1DA .. 0x05D1E0  (3 instructions, 3 dispatch sites: 0x05F738, 0x09A466, 0x09A69A) -- CLASS G01 EXAMPLE ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 is not read by this stub (sites 0x05F738 / 0x09A466 pass the index, 0x09A69A passes 0);  no RAM written
  out  r0; the dispatch sites keep r0 & 0xFF
  this stub at index 0x000:  u8[FFFF8F0B]
  RAM read: FFFF8F0B:u8

MECHANISM

====================================================================================================
==== FUNCTION 0x05D2C0 .. 0x05D2DA  (13 instructions, 3 dispatch sites: 0x05F738, 0x09A466, 0x09A69A) -- CLASS G03 EXAMPLE ====
====================================================================================================
CONTRACT
  in   r4 is not read by this stub (sites 0x05F738 / 0x09A466 pass the index, 0x09A69A passes 0);  no RAM written
  out  r0; the dispatch sites keep r0 & 0xFF
  this stub at index 0x009:  sat8(((f32[FFFF76D4] - 1) + 1) / 0.0078125)
  RAM read: FFFF76D4:f32

MECHANISM
  class G03: sat8(((f32[RAM] + -1) - -1) / k): fr6 = -1 is added to the value AND passed as the offset.
  x + (-1) is rounded to float32 (0x05D2CA) -- TOWARD ZERO, as every FPU step on this chip (FPSCR =
  0x00040001, RM = 01; corrections item 106) -- then the helper computes (that) - (-1) (0x0BE5AA).
  For 0 < x < 0.5, x - 1 lies in (-1, -0.5] where floats are 2^-24 apart; truncation shrinks the
  magnitude, i.e. moves x - 1 UP onto that grid, and the second step is exact (Sterbenz: operands within
  a factor 2).  So x' = x rounded UP to a multiple of 2^-24: x <= x' < x + 2^-24 (asserted exactly).
  For 0.5 <= x <= 2, x - 1 is already exact; every rounding boundary (n - 0.5) * k is below 2; for
  x <= 0 or x > 2 both forms give the same saturated result.  With k = 2^-7 the fdiv is exact and,
  under truncation, "+ 0.5, ftrc" is an exact round-half-up, so both forms are floor(input / k + 0.5)
  of their own input: the pair changes the result exactly when x is less than 2^-24 below a boundary
  b < 0.5, and there it returns the HIGHER count (x = 0.00390619063, 255 floats below 2^-8: this stub
  1, sat8(x / k) 0).  Run on EVERY float32 within 2^-24 of the 256 boundaries: exactly 576 inputs
  differ = 2^-24 / ulp(below b) - 1 per boundary (255 below count 1, 63 below count 2, 31 below each of
  counts 3-4, 15 x 4, 7 x 8, 3 x 16, then one each below counts 33..64); none at or above x = 0.5.
  (The first version of this trace said 319 inputs, "at most 2^-25" below, 127 below count 1: that
  was the same question asked of an emulator that rounded to nearest, which the FPU does not do.)
  1 stub(s) in the class (stub@index):
    05D2C0@009
  pattern: sts.l pr,@-r15 | mov.l @(POOL:RAM),r2 | fmov.s @r2,fr4 | fldi1 fr6 | fneg fr6 | fadd fr6,fr4 | mov.l @(POOL:ROM),r2 | mova @(POOL),r0 | jsr 0x0BE5A8 | fmov.s @r0,fr5 | lds.l @r15+,pr | rts | extu.b r0,r0

EVIDENCE
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py C (symbolic): formula derived by symbolic execution of these bytes, then
  compared with the emulator on 12 seeded-random RAM states for every index that uses the class.
  emulated: emu_stub_tables.py C "rounding_edges": (W) every float32 within 2^-24 of each of the 256
  rounding boundaries, 1536 inputs, and (P) the first version's grid, 40 floats either side of each
  boundary plus the neighbourhood of 0, 20817 inputs.  == sat8(((x - 1) + 1) / 0.0078125) in every one;
  != sat8(x / 0.0078125) for exactly 576 inputs of W (338 of P -- the 40-float grid misses the 215
  differences 41..255 floats below the count-1 boundary, where floats are 2^-32 apart, and the 23 that
  are 41..63 floats below count 2, where they are 2^-30 apart).  Every FPU step in the Python model
  truncates toward zero; the per-boundary counts and x' = ceil(x * 2^24) / 2^24 are asserted, not just
  the total.

     Only 0x05DCAE and 0x05DCCE read r4 (0x05DCB0, 0x05DCD0).  No getter writes RAM or touches a
     peripheral.  [emu C: 848 indices x 7 values of r4, other registers scrambled; static pass; site capture]
 E6  Setter: r4 = value byte; return value unused [0x05F790, 0x09A692].  [emu D, v = 0..255 for all 33 stubs]
 E7  Service 0xB8 at 0x09A628 accepts only: len == 4, index < 848, index == 0x60 (ROM 0x09CA0C, list length 1
     at 0x09A712), setter != 0x05E76A, and getter read-back == value; the read-back call passes r4 = 0.  [emu F]
 E8  The 0x05F08C / 0x05F316 path applies ONLY index < 848 to a 0xB8 request (0x05F27E): no whitelist, no
     null-setter test, and it returns the read-back instead of comparing it.  [0x05F25A..0x05F298, 0x05F4BE..0x05F4EA; emu F]
 E9  486 getter indices -> 0x05D1D0 -> ROM u8[0x064239] = 0xFF; 17 -> 0x05D1CA -> ROM u8[0x064238] = 0x00;
     816 setter indices -> 0x05E76A (rts / nop).  0x05E76A is also the constant 0x09A680 compares against.
 E10 224 stubs / 63 pattern classes as tabulated in PART 3; per-index result in PART 4.  [emu B, C, D]
 E11 0x0BE5A8 / 0x0BE5D8 = clamp(trunc((fr4 - fr6)/fr5 + 0.5), 0, 255 / 65535): the stubs DIVIDE by the pool
     constant, every one of which is a non-zero constant.  The clamp is applied to the INTEGER that ftrc
     returns (0x0BE5BA / 0x0BE5C4), so NaN / infinite / beyond-int32 inputs fall under N8.
     0x0BE598 / 0x0BE588 = fr5 + fr4 * float(u16 / u8 of r4).  [emu E]
 E12 Multi-byte values occupy consecutive indices, high byte first: 0x00E/0x00F, 0x013/0x014, 0x018/0x019,
     0x182/0x183 (halves of one sat16), 0x10E/0x10F (u16[0xFFFF3140]), 0x200..0x203 (u32[0xFFFF69B0]).  [PART 4]
 E13 Indices 0x001..0x005 return ROM 0x0D97F0..0x0D97F4 = 8A 12 58 70 07, the only occurrence of that string
     in the image and equal to the definition XML's <ecuid>8A12587007</ecuid>.  [emu G]
 E14 Indices served by 0x05DCAE read u8[0xFFFFAD52 + k] and those served by 0x05DCCE read u8[0xFFFF3B06 + 2k],
     with k = ROM u8[0x065CBC + index - 0x8E]; 79 indices each, spread over 0x08E..0x2C3.  [PART 4]
 E15 Every setter that writes RAM writes a location the getter at the same index reads (29 indices).  [emu F pairing]
 E16 0x05F758 raises the interrupt mask (0x0BE81C(sp, 0xE0)) around every write; when flag bit 7 is set it
     sets bit 0 of the word register 0xFFFFEC0A before the write, and it clears that bit afterwards in
     EVERY case (0x05F7A6..0x05F7AC are not conditional).  [0x05F764..0x05F7B2; emu F]
 E17 Against rom/ae5l600l.bin: both tables, all stubs, 0x05F08C..0x05F80F, 0x09A628..0x09A75F, the record
     tables and the transport helpers 0x098686..0x098727 / 0x09CA0C..0x09D0B7 are byte-identical.  Of the
     code in this file only 0x09A3C2..0x09A473 differs (175 of 178 bytes).  (0x098686..0x09D0B7 as a whole
     differs in 209 bytes: those 175 plus 34 in 0x09A788..0x09A80D, one-byte 00 / 01 cells the definition
     XML names as DTC enable flags, e.g. 0x09A788 "(P0458) EVAP PURGE VALVE CIRCUIT LOW" -- calibration
     data, not part of any function listed here.)
 E18 The FF / F2 / F4 items of 0x09A3AC read 1 / 2 / 4 consecutive bytes, byte by byte and ascending, at
     0xFFFF0000 | (b1 << 8) | b2.  Neither the address nor the byte count is checked: the range is all of
     0xFFFF0000..0xFFFFFFFF (RAM 0xFFFF0000..0xFFFFBFFF and everything above it), and r1 runs on past
     0xFFFFFFFF.  [0x09A446..0x09A456; emu F "tagged items": FF EC 0A, F2 EC 0A, FF C0 00, F4 FF FE]
 E19 0x09A3AC stores reply data from 0xFFFFB3F4 through a pointer it never compares [0x09A418, 0x09A44E..
     0x09A450, 0x09A46C..0x09A46E].  1 byte per table / FF item, 2 / 4 per F2 / F4 item, up to 84 items
     [0x09A3C6..0x09A3E8]: at most 336 bytes, to 0xFFFFB543.  Data byte 88 (from 0) lands on u16[0xFFFFB44C]
     [used at 0x09A6D2], byte 92 on the 0x09CAEA mode byte u8[0xFFFFB450] [0x09CAF4..0x09CAFC], byte 96 on the
     ring record at 0xFFFFB454 [0x09CD58, 0x09CD04].  The stock code stores indexed by item number and
     cannot exceed 84 bytes.  [emu F "tagged items": 22 / 23 / 24 / 25 / 84 x F4]
 E20 Transport under both handlers is a byte ring: record 0xFFFFB454 = {u16 write index, u16 read index,
     u16 mark, 601 data bytes}, gated by u8[0xFFFFB6DF] == id.  0x09D052 -> 0x09CD50 copies N bytes out at
     the read index (0 when nothing is unread); 0x09CFEE -> 0x09CCF8 appends at the write index (dropping
     at mark, no range check on the index before the store); 0x09D084 -> 0x09CC44 sets write index =
     mark + 1; 0x098686(id, code, 2) = reset + append 7F id code + 8 x 00 + 0x09CABE(&{id, 3, 8, 0}).
     With all of them run for real every handler result above is unchanged.  [emu F "transport"]
 E21 0x09A3AC consumes min(len, 253) request bytes even when it then refuses the length [0x09A402 before
     0x09A406]; 0x09A628 refuses a wrong length WITHOUT reading the request [0x09A63A..0x09A63E].
     0x09A3AC never examines the byte in front of the items.  [emu F]
 E22 Any non-zero u8[0xFFFFB450] makes 0x09CAEA return 2 without calling a handler for every id in the
     selected table: 0x003BF4 gives 1 << n (0 for n >= 32) and every record mask is 1.  [emu F, 0..255]
 E23 Parser 0x05F08C sets GBR = 0xFFFF8F34 [0x05F098..0x05F09A].  On its 0xA8 branch 0x05F2FC copies bit 0
     of u8[0xFFFF8F42] into bit 15 of the flags word and the branch then sets bit 12; bit 15 is cleared
     on the 0xB8 and refusal paths.  [0x05F1B4, 0x05F2FC..0x05F314, 0x05F1E4..0x05F1EA, 0x05F2E0..0x05F2E4; emu F]
 E24 Index 0x009 is sat8(((f32[0xFFFF76D4] - 1) + 1) / 0.0078125) with float32 arithmetic: the x - 1 at
     0x05D2CA is truncated toward zero (FPSCR RM = 01, corrections item 106), the + 1 in the helper is
     exact.  It equals sat8(f32[0xFFFF76D4] / 0.0078125) except for exactly 576 float32 inputs, all with
     0 < x < 0.5 and less than 2^-24 (5.96e-8) below a rounding boundary, where it is one count HIGHER.
     [0x05D2C6..0x05D2CA, 0x0BE5AA; the bound by the argument under class G03; emu C "rounding_edges":
     every float32 within 2^-24 of all 256 boundaries.  Superseded figure: 319 / 2^-25, from a
     round-to-nearest emulator -- see REVIEW HISTORY, RZ re-run.]
 E25 The scale index 0x015 divides by is 0x3EA0A0A0 = 0.31372547 [0x05D3EC -> 0x05D5C4] and index 0x029's is
     0x3EC8C8C8 = 0.39215684 [0x05D502 -> 0x05D604]: 80/255 and 100/255, each one float32 step BELOW the
     nearest value -- i.e. exactly 80/255 and 100/255 truncated toward zero, the FPU's own rounding mode
     (asserted; how the constants were generated is not in the ROM).  Index 0x015 therefore returns 255
     for f32[0xFFFF62D4] = 80.0.
 E26 The two flag readers getter 0x05E2EA calls, 0x02E7D8 / 0x02E7E2, return (u8[0xFFFF7234] == 1) /
     (u8[0xFFFF7235] == 1).  No other callee of any of the 224 stubs is missing from this file (30 distinct
     callees, swept mechanically).  [emu B "callee_sweep", emu E]

NOT ESTABLISHED (and what would settle it)
---------------
 N1  That ids 0xA8 / 0xB8 are Subaru "SSM read address / write address", and which physical link each of the
     two request paths serves.  The ROM shows only the id bytes, the replies id + 0x40 (0xE8, 0xF8) and, for
     the 0x09xxxx path, a negative reply built by 0x098686 and 8 zero bytes appended from ROM 0x09B770.
     Settle: trace 0x09CA18 (caller of 0x09CAEA) and 0x05EC76 / 0x05ED62 / 0x05EE9C back to the receive ISR
     and its peripheral.
 N2  What any index or RAM address physically IS.  PART 4 gives address, width, offset and scale only.
     logs/logcfg.txt (in repo, user-written) names 20 of the indices.  For 8 of them its scalingrpn is the
     exact inverse of the getter; for 11 the logged value is the RAM input times a factor and/or plus an
     offset (B10) -- that the factors are unit conversions is a reading, not a byte fact; for one, Throttle
     (id 0x015), the logged value is 1.25 x f32[0xFFFF62D4] (E25): the rpn assumes 255 counts = 100, the
     getter produces 255 counts at 80.0.  Whether that float is in a unit whose full scale is 80, or the
     logged "Throttle %" is simply 25 % high, is NOT established.  Agreement between two artefacts is not an
     identity proof either way.  The definition XMLs hold no diagnostic parameter-id list at all; <ecuid>
     (E13) is the only definition datum these tables can be checked against.  Settle per variable: find
     the WRITER (scripts/mapping/find_writers.py) and decode it.
 N3  0x00DCE4, which 0x09CABE jumps to with (2, 1, &{id, length, x, y}); what x means (0 after a positive
     reply, 8 after 0x098686).  Who puts the request into the ring, sets mark and binds u8[0xFFFFB6DF] to
     the id: 0x09CA18 (the caller of 0x09CAEA) and what is upstream of it.  E20 establishes what the
     helpers DO with the ring, not that the ring content is the bus request.
 N4  Whether anything upstream of 0x05F08C (0x05EC76 / 0x05ED62 / 0x05EE9C, not traced) gates a 0xB8 request
     before the unrestricted write path at 0x05F4BE runs, and whether that path is reachable on this car.
 N5  What register 0xFFFFEC0A is and why bit 0 brackets writes when flag bit 7 is set; the address-range rules
     of 0x05F62A that set flag bits 4, 5, 7 (it compares against 0xFFFF2000, 0xFFFF2004, 0xFFFF203C,
     0xFFFF3FE4, 0xFFFFBFA0, 0x000C0000, 0x000FFB80 -- listing not included here).
 N6  The 0xA0 / 0xB0 / 0xBF / 0x9F branches of 0x05F08C and the 0x05F372 / 0x05F3D2 / 0x05F43C / 0x05F4EC
     fragments of 0x05F316 (they use other tables: 0x065EF4, 0x066094).
 N7  The meaning of the mode byte u8[0xFFFFB450] and of the selector argument of 0x09CAEA.
 N8  Getter results for NaN / infinite / out-of-int32 float inputs ON SILICON.  They are now exercised
     (emu C "extreme_floats", emu E) but under the ftrc rule scripts/sh2e_emu.py implements (NaN and
     negative overflow -> 0x80000000 -> result 0; positive overflow -> 0x7FFFFFFF -> result 255 / 65535).
     Under that rule every formula in PART 4 still holds.  Under truncation a FINITE overflow inside the
     helper (e.g. 3.4e38 / 0.0078125) gives +-FLT_MAX, not +-Inf; ftrc then saturates the same way, so
     the results above do not depend on it.  Settle: the SH7058 hardware manual's FTRC special-case
     table, or a logged NaN.
 N9  Why the 0x09A628 read-back passes r4 = 0 (harmless today: index 0x60 does not use r4).
 N10 Whether indices with a constant getter (0x05D1D0 = 0xFF and the "sat8(0...)" stubs) are "unsupported"
     in any protocol sense; the bytes only show that they return a constant.
 N11 Whether the reply overrun of E19 is ever reached, and what follows when it is.  It needs more than 88
     data bytes from F2 / F4 items in one request; how the logger builds its requests (one FF item per
     byte, or F2 / F4 items for 16-bit / float parameters) is not in the ROM or the repo.  What follows the
     overwrite (where 0x09CCF8 puts a reply once its write index holds reply data; what the ECU does once
     0x09CAEA refuses every id) was not traced.  Settle: capture one request frame from the logger; trace
     0x09CA18.
 N12 What a tagged read returns from 0xFFFFC000..0xFFFFDFFF, and whether reading an on-chip register through
     E18 has side effects (status flags cleared by a read).  The emulator has nothing mapped there.
 N13 What reads bit 15 of the flags word u16[0xFFFF9048] that E23 sets from bit 0 of the 0xA8 request's
     first byte.

####################################################################################################
# COMPARISON WITH EXISTING CLAIMS   (made AFTER the blind trace above; nothing above was changed to fit)
####################################################################################################

The premise "nobody has looked at these" (docs/corrections.md:6224) is itself wrong.  The repo already holds
a largely correct account of the getter table (docs/ssm-read-patch.md, corrections.md "Instrument A"), a
wrong one (disassembly/analysis/diag_dispatch_table_analysis.txt and its Ghidra labels) and a wrong count
(function_census.json).  None of them was opened before PART 1..5 were written.

A. CONTRADICTED OR NOT SUPPORTED BY THE BYTES
---------------------------------------------
 A1  docs/corrections.md:6224-6225, docs/analysis-plan.md:127-128, disassembly/maps/function_census.json
     (summary.dispatch_tables): "0x06423C (1,696 code pointers) and 0x064F7C (848)", "nobody has looked at",
     four dispatch sites.  WRONG: 848 + 848 (E1); a fifth site 0x09A466 exists (E2); function 0x09A3AC has
     151 instructions, not the census's 140.  [Census regenerated later on 2026-11-27: it now lists the five
     sites and 151 instructions; its 1,696 for 0x06423C is still the adjacent-run reading E1 refutes.  The
     docs/corrections.md and docs/analysis-plan.md wording was not re-checked here.]
 A2  disassembly/analysis/diag_dispatch_table_analysis.txt:16-24, 41-46, 51, 66, 72-73, 89, 105-112, 126-134,
     162-164, 178-181, 188-202 and disassembly/ghidra/ImportAE5L600L.java:7290-7330 ([TRACED] labels
     diag_monitor_dispatch_table, diag_monitor_noop, diag_monitor_status_read, diag_monitor_cal_read_D97Fx ...):
     "597-entry table, 12 bytes/entry, three function pointers [precondition, main, output] per diagnostic
     monitor", "0x05E76A = disabled monitor (270 entries)", "0x05D1D0 status read (159 entries)", "0x0D97F0..4
     are DTC enable bytes", "the complex handlers are the real monitor logic with descriptor lookups via
     0xBE5A8 and conditional DTC state updates", "a dispatcher near 0x058xxx calls the three pointers in
     sequence", "replace a pointer with 0x05E76A to disable a monitor".
     WRONG throughout for 0x06423C..0x065CBB: every one of the five dispatch sites scales the index by 4
     (shll2 at 0x05F732, 0x05F788, 0x09A462, 0x09A674; the saved index*4 at 0x09A696), there is no 12-byte
     stride and no caller that walks the table; 0x05E76A is the null SETTER (816 entries); 0x05D1D0 is the
     default GETTER returning 0xFF (486 entries); 0x0D97F0..4 hold the ECU id 8A 12 58 70 07 (E13); 0x0BE5A8
     is a float -> byte conversion, not a descriptor lookup; no getter writes RAM (E5).  Putting 0x05E76A
     into a getter slot would make that read address return whatever r0 held -- it disables nothing.
     (What lies at 0x064100..0x064237 was not examined here.)
 A3  ImportAE5L600L.java:2139-2144 [TRACED] fmac_interp_uint16 (0x0BE598) / fmac_interp_uint8 (0x0BE588):
     "fr0 = fr4 + fr3*(fr5-fr4). Core 1D interpolation".  WRONG formula: 0BE59E fmov fr4,fr0 / 0BE5A0 fmac
     fr0,fr3,fr5 / 0BE5A4 fmov fr5,fr0 gives fr0 = fr5 + fr4*float(raw).  A linear raw -> engineering
     conversion (factor fr4, offset fr5); nothing is blended.  [emu E, 80 cases]
 A4  ImportAE5L600L.java:615-616 [TRACED] uint8_unpack (0x0BE980): "Extracts size from packed hi:~lo byte
     pair".  WRONG: it returns a CHECK byte, 0 exactly when the low byte is the complement of the high byte
     (0BE980..0BE98C); the caller 0x05DF04 then reads the value itself.  [emu SPECIAL, 264 words]
 A5  ImportAE5L600L.java:2130-2136 [TRACED] axis_frac_to_uint16 / axis_frac_to_uint8 (0x0BE5D8 / 0x0BE5A8):
     the arithmetic in the comment matches the bytes, but "float axis position -> index+fraction for table
     lookups" is not what the 80 stubs here that call them do: they convert a physical float to a scaled
     unsigned byte/word for a diagnostic reply.  Name MISLEADING for these callers.
 A6  ImportAE5L600L.java:2381 dtc_counter_update (0x09D052), :2249 dtc_scan_loop (0x09CFEE), :2379
     dtc_status_check (0x098686), :2385 dtc_freeze_frame (0x09CABE), all [UNVERIFIED].  NOT SUPPORTED: in both
     handlers they are called as (service id, buffer, length) to fetch the request [0x09A402, 0x09A648], to
     append reply bytes [0x09A49E, 0x09A4A8, 0x09A6DE, 0x09A6E8], to send a 3-byte negative reply
     [0x09A482, 0x09A6B8; 0x0986A2 loads 0x7F as its first byte] and to submit {id, length, 0, 0}
     [0x09A4C2, 0x09A704].  Nothing at these call sites concerns trouble codes.
     Since decoded (PART 1, transport helpers), which turns "not supported" into WRONG for three of them:
     0x09D052 does not "increment fault detection counters" -- it copies N bytes out of the ring at
     0xFFFFB454 when u8[0xFFFFB6DF] equals the id; 0x09CFEE does not "iterate DTC entries" -- it appends N
     bytes to that ring (its loop bound is the immediate 1, and u8[0xFFFFB6DF] is compared with the id, not
     used as an "enable"); 0x098686 does not "return fault state" -- it appends 7F, id, code and has no
     return value.  0x09CABE is a 7-instruction tail jump to 0x00DCE4; what that does is not traced, so
     "freeze frame capture" stays unsupported rather than refuted.
 A7  ImportAE5L600L.java:8064 cl_ol_scheduler_workspace (0xFFFF8F28), :8070 gbr_sched_8F34 (0xFFFF8F34), :7228
     [TRACED] sched_task_dispatch_state (0xFFFF903C); disassembly/maps/ram_reference.txt:297.  NOT SUPPORTED:
     0xFFFF8F28 is the stub-table index word (0x05F4D6, 0x05F5E8 write; 0x05F72E, 0x05F784 read), 0xFFFF8F34
     is the GBR base of the request parser 0x05F08C, 0xFFFF903C is the 4-byte reply buffer 0x05F718 fills
     (0x05F756).  Nothing in these functions schedules tasks or concerns closed/open loop.
 A8  RAM names in disassembly/maps/ram_reference.txt / docs/verification-status.json that the getter's TYPE
     contradicts or that hide what the address holds (all DISASM-ONLY, i.e. never cross-checked):
       0xFFFF4024 ATU_primary_ctrl (ram_reference.txt:204) -- WRONG: a RAM word that index 0x101 scales exactly
           like the other 16-bit analog counts (x 5/65536 / 0.02, same as 0xFFFF4042 at index 0x01D); on-chip
           registers live at 0xFFFFE000 and above; 0xFFFF4024 is RAM.
       0xFFFF7E90 task50_gbr_base (:233), 0xFFFF5C98 peripheral_control_GBR (:287), 0xFFFF91C4 sched_queue_base
           (:333), 0xFFFF9094 sched_task_GBR (:227), 0xFFFF83AC idle_workspace_GBR (:260) -- MISLEADING: each
           address holds a value in its own right: f32 at indices 0x011, 0x030, 0x03E; f32 compared with a
           +-0.390625 band at 0x066; a flag byte read bit-wise at 0x0E1 (and written by setter 0x0E1).
       0xFFFF20CC system_state_flags_A (:285) -- read as an f32 and converted with sat8 at index 0x10A.
       0xFFFF653C o2_sensor_voltage (:257) -- index 0x046 multiplies it by 128; logcfg.txt:10-12 reads the same
           id as x/128*14.7.  A ratio near 1, not a voltage (the label's own text hedges "or lambda").
       0xFFFF40C8 cam_angle_sensor (:255) vs logs/logcfg.txt:21-23, which logs id 0x044 (this address, scale 1)
           as "EGT".  Two claims in the repo disagree; the bytes do not say which is right.
 A9  docs/corrections.md:1143 and disassembly/maps/ram_reference.txt:134-135: "0x0BE5D8 / 0x0BE5A8 are both clamp_u16",
     "each getter is a leaf that loads ONE RAM address into FR4".  IMPRECISE: 0x0BE5A8 clamps to 255 (0BE5AC
     loads 0x00FF); only 45 of the 848 indices have that one-float shape -- the rest are constants, plain
     bytes, bit-packed status bytes, two-float sums/differences and ADC words.  corrections.md:1155 lists
     index 0x24 with "0 / 7.5006": its getter 0x05D4BC computes ((f32[0xFFFF620C] - f32[0xFFFF689C]) +
     960.08) / 7.5006, i.e. a DIFFERENCE of two variables with offset -960.08.
 A10 docs/ssm-read-patch.md:7 "inside the function at 0x09A3B0" (entry is 0x09A3AC; already recorded as
     corrections item 101, the doc line is still stale); :91 "Untagged entries fall through to the stock
     getter-table path, so nothing breaks" -- IMPRECISE: the stock code also rejected a request whose first
     byte is non-zero (stock 0x09A408..0x09A40C); the patched code never looks at that byte.
     :99 calls 0xFFFF65FC "(load)", which CLAUDE.md corrections item 9 says is vehicle speed (doc vs doc;
     not adjudicated here).
     :45, :48, :68, :87 "real RAM reads", "read RAM directly", "copy r4+1 bytes from RAM", "every tag lands
     on RAM base 0xFFFF0000" -- IMPRECISE: the low 16 bits are unconstrained, so the tagged path reads any
     address 0xFFFF0000..0xFFFFFFFF, on-chip registers included, and nothing bounds the reply (E18, E19).
     :103-106 "Every extended channel in logs/ -- AFC, AFL, FLKC, FBKC, avcs, wgdc, IPW, IDC, KNOCK_FLAG --
     is a direct RAM read with no stock getter.  Without these 178 bytes none of them are loggable" --
     WRONG for six of the nine as logs/logcfg.txt stands: AFC (id 0x009), AFL (0x00A), FLKC (0x199), avcs
     (0x03D), wgdc (0x030) and IPW (0x020) are table indices, served by getters 0x05D2C0, 0x05D2DA,
     0x05E5F4, 0x05D6B8, 0x05D54A, 0x05D478 that are byte-identical in the stock image (E17).  Only the
     0xFFxxxx ids (FFB, RQTQ, Trgt_Boost, CL/OL, FBKC, tdi, Tdp, KNOCK_FLAG) go through the patched bytes.
 A11 CLAUDE.md:72 "the RAM addresses this project logs (FFFF6624 RPM, FFFF6350 ECT, FFFF63F8 load)".
     NOT SUPPORTED by logs/logcfg.txt as it stands: ECT is logged as id 0x000008 (logcfg.txt:159-161), whose
     getter 0x05D204 reads f32[0xFFFF4144]; no logcfg entry reads 0xFFFF6350 or 0xFFFF63F8.  Likewise the
     logged "IAT" (id 0x012) reads 0xFFFF4128, "MPH" (id 0x010) reads 0xFFFF6600 and "Throttle" (id 0x015)
     reads 0xFFFF62D4 -- not 0xFFFF6364, 0xFFFF65FC, 0xFFFF62DC that CLAUDE.md gives for IAT, speed and
     throttle.  These may be different stages of the same quantities; that is NOT established either way.
 A12 disassembly/analysis/hole9_afl_trace.txt:2275-2276 (untracked working file; line numbers as of this run)
     "(12 instructions, 2 callers) Callers: 0x05F718,
     0x09A628" for getter 0x05DCFA.  IMPRECISE: three dispatch sites reach every getter (E2).
 A13 logs/logcfg.txt:75-78 "P13 - Throttle Opening Angle(%)", paramid 0x000015, scalingrpn x,100,*,255,/.
     NOT the inverse of getter 0x05D3E2, which divides f32[0xFFFF62D4] by 80/255 (E25): the logged value is
     1.25 x that float, whereas the identically scaled "Accelerator" (id 0x029, logcfg.txt:70-73) is logged
     1 : 1 because its getter divides by 100/255.  Two artefacts disagree by a factor; which one is "right"
     depends on the unit of f32[0xFFFF62D4], which the bytes do not give (N2).
 A14 ImportAE5L600L.java:7461-7464 check_fuel_7234 (0x02E7D8) / check_fuel_7235 (0x02E7E2), [UNVERIFIED].

     The "fuel" in the names is NOT SUPPORTED by anything decoded here: the only caller in this trace is

B. CONFIRMED BY THE BYTES
-------------------------
 B1  docs/ssm-read-patch.md:29-41: bound 848 (word at 0x09A4DA), getter table 0x06423C, index*4, one byte per

     critical_section_enter / _exit (0x0BE81C / 0x0BE82C): descriptions match the bytes.

 B10 logs/logcfg.txt has 20 (not 19) parameters whose id is a table index.  For 19 of them the scalingrpn
