     The twentieth, Throttle 0x015, is NOT consistent (x 1.25): see A13.
 B11 ram_reference.txt / verification-status.json names whose TYPE and scale the getters are consistent with:
     rpm_current 0xFFFF6624, mass_airflow_gps 0xFFFF63C4, manifold_pressure_map 0xFFFF620C, battery_voltage
     0xFFFF4130, accel_pedal_angle 0xFFFF64D8, maf_sensor_adc_counts 0xFFFF4042, ratio_0to1_69F0 0xFFFF69F0
     (index 0x032 multiplies by 100 then divides by 0.392: full scale at 1.0), diag_monitor_status_bytes
     0xFFFF61CC (indices 0x133..0x136 return 0xFFFF61CC..CF as four plain bytes).  Consistency of type and
     scale only -- per CLAUDE.md rule 7 this does not prove a name.

C. DID THE COMPARISON CHANGE THE BLIND READING?
-----------------------------------------------
 No.  The one place the existing material adds something the blind pass could not have is naming: the repo
 already treats ids 0xA8/0xB8 as SSM and logcfg.txt names 20 indices.  Those are recorded above as claims.
 The blind reading of 0x09A3AC, the tag bytes and the getter formulas agrees with docs/ssm-read-patch.md and
 corrections.md "Instrument A"; the blind reading of the table SHAPE disagrees with
 diag_dispatch_table_analysis.txt, and the bytes (index*4 at five sites, bound 848 at four) settle it.

####################################################################################################
# REVIEW HISTORY   (2026-11-27: blind trace -> adversarial review -> this deciding decode)
####################################################################################################

"T" = the first version of this file and of scripts/trace/emu_stub_tables.py; "R" = the adversarial reviewer.
Each item was decided again from bytes and the emulator; neither side was presumed right.  Instruction lines
below are printed by fn_dump and are verifier-checked like every other listing line.

 H1  T (claim C08): tagged items FF / F2 / F4 return "1 / 2 / 4 bytes of RAM".
     R: the range is 0xFFFF0000..0xFFFFFFFF, and nothing bounds it.
     R IS RIGHT.  Only bits 16..19 are forced; b1 and b2 are used as they come and r1 is never compared:
09A446: E00F  mov #15,r0                    ; r0 = 15
09A448: 4028  shll16 r0                     ; r0 = 0x000F0000
09A44A: 210B  or r0,r1                      ; r1 = 0xFFFF0000 | (b1 << 8) | b2
09A44C: 6014  mov.b @r1+,r0                 ; read at r1, post-increment, no bound
     emulated: item FF EC 0A -> ONE read, of 0xFFFFEC0A (register space); FF C0 00 -> read of 0xFFFFC000;
     F4 FF FE -> bytes of 0xFFFFFFFE, 0xFFFFFFFF, 0x00000000, 0x00000001.  The byte counts and the ascending
     order were right in both readings.  Repaired: RESULT 8, the 0x09A3AC contract, E18, N12.

 H2  T (claim C23): "getter calling convention: r4 = index".
     R: not at one of the three dispatch sites.
     R IS RIGHT, and T's own 0x09A628 section already said so while its summary did not:
05F73A: 642D  extu.w r2,r4                  ; site 0x05F738: r4 = index
09A468: 641D  extu.w r1,r4                  ; site 0x09A466: r4 = index
09A69C: E400  mov #0,r4                     ; site 0x09A69A: r4 = 0
     emulated: r4 captured inside the getter at each site = index, index, 0.
     The second half of the claim ("only 0x05DCAE and 0x05DCCE read r4") STANDS and is now shown three ways:
     T's 4-value test, 848 indices x 7 values of r4 with every other register scrambled, and a static pass
     over every path with callees inlined, which finds exactly two reads of the entry value:
05DCB0: 6643  mov r4,r6                     ; stub 0x05DCAE
05DCD0: 6643  mov r4,r6                     ; stub 0x05DCCE
     Repaired: RESULT 3, E5, the "in" line of all 53 per-class contracts.

 H3  T (claim C36): index 0x009 = sat8(f32[0xFFFF76D4] / 0.0078125), "the -1 cancels".
     R: only up to float32 rounding; 231 of 20,736 boundary probes differ.
     R IS RIGHT that they do not cancel.  The stub adds -1 and rounds; the helper subtracts -1:
05D2C6: F69D  fldi1 fr6                     ; fr6 = 1.0
05D2C8: F64D  fneg fr6                      ; fr6 = -1.0
05D2CA: F460  fadd fr6,fr4                  ; fr4 = x - 1, rounded
0BE5AA: F461  fsub fr6,fr4                  ; in the helper: fr4 = (x - 1) + 1, exact for 0 < x <= 2 (Sterbenz)
     emulated here: T's grid (40 floats either side of each of the 256 boundaries, plus around 0, 20817
     probes): ROM == sat8(((x - 1) + 1) / 0.0078125) in all of them; ROM != sat8(x / 0.0078125) in 231
     (the same count R reports; x = 0.00390624069 -> ROM 1, simplified 0).
     R's bound "within about 6e-8" is true but loose, and "can move the result by one count" leaves the
     direction open.  The bytes give more: 0x05D2CA rounds x - 1 onto a 2^-24 grid (error <= 2^-25) and
     0x0BE5AA's subtraction is exact (Sterbenz), so the result can only move UP, and only for 0 < x < 0.5
     at most 2^-25 = 2.98e-8 below a boundary.  Running EVERY float32 within 2^-24 of all 256 boundaries
     (1536) finds exactly 319 such inputs; the 231 of both T's and R's grids is a probe count, not the
     total (the grid stops 40 floats down; below the count-1 boundary differences reach 128 floats down).
     PART 4 already printed the exact form; the class table and the G03 mechanism line claimed equality.
     Repaired there and in E24; the script's rounding_edges() now runs the complete window.
     SUPERSEDED FIGURES (RZ RE-RUN below): every number in this item -- 231, 319, "error <= 2^-25",
     "128 floats down" -- came from an emulator that rounded to nearest.  The FPU truncates toward zero;
     re-run, the grid gives 338, the complete window 576, the bound is "less than 2^-24" (so R's "about
     6e-8" was the right size after all) and differences reach 255 floats below the count-1 boundary.
     The direction (+1, ROM higher, only for 0 < x < 0.5) and the exact form are unchanged.

 H4  T (claim C47): "all 19 parameters in logs/logcfg.txt ... inverse of (or unit-consistent with)" the getter.
     R: 20 parameters, and Throttle (id 0x015) is not: its getter divides by 80/255, its rpn is x*100/255.
     R IS RIGHT on both counts (T's own script printed 20 lines).  Deciding bytes:
05D3EC: C775  mova @(0x05D5C4),r0           ; -> 0x05D5C4: long 0x3EA0A0A0 f32 0.3137255 -- index 0x015: scale 0x3EA0A0A0 = 80/255 truncated
05D502: C740  mova @(0x05D604),r0           ; -> 0x05D604: long 0x3EC8C8C8 f32 0.3921568 -- index 0x029: scale 0x3EC8C8C8 = 100/255 truncated
     so logged Throttle = 1.25 x f32[0xFFFF62D4] while logged Accelerator = f32[0xFFFF64D8].
     R's own wording ("19 are inverses up to a unit conversion") is looser than the bytes allow: 8 are exact
     inverses and 11 carry a factor or offset (B10).  Calling those factors unit conversions is a reading.
     Repaired: B10, N2, E25; the script now computes "logged = slope x input + intercept" for all 20.

 H5  R (missed): 0x09A3AC has no bound on reply bytes; F2 / F4 items overrun into u16[0xFFFFB44C],
     u8[0xFFFFB450] and the ring header at 0xFFFFB454.
     R IS RIGHT.  The store cursor is loaded once and only stored through and incremented:
09A418: DA33  mov.l @(0x09A4E8),r10         ; = 0xFFFFB3F4 RAM -- r10 = reply data cursor
09A44E: 2A00  mov.b r0,@r10                 ; store
09A450: 7A01  add #1,r10                    ; advance; no compare anywhere
     emulated: 22 / 23 / 24 / 25 / 84 x F4 store 88 / 92 / 96 / 100 / 336 bytes from 0xFFFFB3F4; the three
     locations are overwritten from 23 / 24 / 25 items on; any non-zero u8[0xFFFFB450] makes 0x09CAEA return
     2 for every listed id (swept 0..255).  R's "not reached by the current logcfg.txt (only FF-tagged
     one-byte items)" is NOT adopted: how the logger encodes 16-bit and float parameters is not in the ROM
     or the repo (N11).  Repaired: RESULT 8, the 0x09A3AC contract, E19, E22.

 H6  R (missed): the tagged path reads the on-chip register area and wraps past 0xFFFFFFFF.  = H1.  RIGHT.

 H7  R (missed): 0x05F2FC is called on the 0xA8 and 0xA0 branches of 0x05F08C and writes the flags word.
     R IS RIGHT.  Listed and emulated now (byte 0..255 x 5 flag words; and through 0x05F08C, which T had
     not emulated at all).  Bit 15 of the flags word = bit 0 of u8[0xFFFF8F42].  E23, N13.

 H8  R (missed): callees 0x02E7D8 / 0x02E7E2 of getter 0x05E2EA are not listed.
     R IS RIGHT.  Listed in PART 2.  A sweep of every callee of all 224 stubs (30 distinct) finds no other
     one missing (emu B "callee_sweep").  E26.

 H9  R (missed): the transport helpers T stubbed are short, run in the emulator, and with them real every
     handler claim still holds.
     R IS RIGHT.  0x09D052 / 0x09CD50 / 0x09CFEE / 0x09CCF8 / 0x09D084 / 0x09CC44 / 0x098686 / 0x09CABE are
     listed in PART 1; the three ring routines match Python models; both handlers were re-run with only
     0x00DCE4 stubbed.  R's two side findings also hold (E21).  One limit on "the stub assumption is now
     evidence": what is proved is WHAT 0x09D052 copies (unread ring bytes, gated on the bound id), not
     that the ring holds the bus request -- that is set by 0x09CA18 and upstream, still not traced (N3).

 H10 R (missed): the 0x05F08C listing omitted the pair that sets GBR.  R IS RIGHT.  Now listed:
05F098: D043  mov.l @(0x05F1A8),r0          ; = 0xFFFF8F34 RAM -- r0 = GBR base
05F09A: 401E  ldc r0,gbr                    ; GBR = 0xFFFF8F34 -- every gbr+N annotation of 0x05F08C rests on this

 H11 R (file problems), each checked:
     * "verify with --rom": RIGHT.  Without it 89 lines of T's file (97 of this one) were skipped, not
       checked.  Header now says so.  This file: 2655 checked / 0 mismatch / 0 skipped with --rom;
       2558 / 0 / 97 without.
     * extent() blind spot in the script: RIGHT.  A delay slot that is also a branch target was never
       walked: extent(0x09A3AC) gave 140, not 151.  Fixed and asserted.  The 224 stubs were unaffected
       (2357 instructions before and after).  scripts/mapping/fn_dump.py and function_census.json have the
       same blind spot at 0x09A45A; they are not this trace's files and were not changed (A1).
       [Since fixed in both: fn_dump and the regenerated census give 0x09A3AC 151 instructions.]
     * "19" should be 20: RIGHT (H4).  "559 constants" should be 560: RIGHT -- index 0x0DF (stub 0x05DEC6)
       reads nothing and returns 0 but was tallied "dynamic".  Now "const"; 288 + 560 = 848 asserted.
     * pool slot cited for getter 0x05D1E0: RIGHT.  0x05D298 holds 0xFFFF8F0B (getter 0x05D1DA, index 0);
       getter 0x05D1E0 (index 1) loads 0x05D29C = 0x000D97F0.  The listings were always correct; the slip
       was in T's claim list.
05D1E0: D22E  mov.l @(0x05D29C),r2          ; = 0x000D97F0 CAL -- index 0x001
     * hole9_afl_trace.txt line numbers: RIGHT, now :2275-2276.
     * "docs/corrections.md:6224 -- the sentence begins at :6223": R IS WRONG as the working tree stands;
       `grep -n "Two indexed pointer tables" docs/corrections.md` gives 6224.  Citation left as T had it.
     * sat_model could not evaluate NaN / Inf: RIGHT.  It now mirrors the emulator's ftrc rule and NaN,
       +-Inf, +-3.4e38 and beyond-int32 values are run through both helpers and every float-input getter.
       This is the EMULATOR's rule, not silicon: N8 stays open, narrower.
     * sh2e_emu.py fdiv returns Inf for NaN / 0: confirmed by reading it (the fm == 0 branch takes
       copysign(inf, fn) for any fn != 0, NaN included).  Not this trace's file; not changed.  It cannot
       touch a result here: every scale handed to 0x0BE5A8 / 0x0BE5D8 is a non-zero constant (asserted).

 Also corrected while repairing, raised by neither side:
     * E17 said "in this area only 0x09A3C2..0x09A473 differs" from stock.  For the CODE listed here that is
       right, but between 0x098686 and 0x09D0B7 the 20.19c bin also differs in 34 bytes at 0x09A788..
       0x09A80D -- 00 / 01 DTC enable cells named by the definition XML (calibration, not code).  Reworded.
     * The G03 class line gave the 0x009 discrepancy as "within ~6e-8"; the provable bound is 2^-25 and
       the direction is always +1 (H3).  [2^-25 SUPERSEDED: that was the round-to-nearest bound.  Under
       the chip's truncation the bound is < 2^-24 = 5.96e-8 -- RZ RE-RUN.]
     * E16 read as if bit 0 of 0xFFFFEC0A were cleared only when flag bit 7 had set it; the clear at
       0x05F7A6..0x05F7AC is unconditional (the 0x05F758 contract already said so).
     * the script described stub 0x05DEC6 only as "bits: all 0 for every input tried"; it is straight-line
       code with no input at all.
     * docs/ssm-read-patch.md:103-106 (comparison A10): six of the nine channels it calls "direct RAM read
       with no stock getter" are logged through stock getters.

 RZ RE-RUN  (2026-11-27, after corrections item 106: the SH7058 FPU rounds TOWARD ZERO)
     scripts/sh2e_emu.py had rounded every single-precision result to nearest.  Reset loads FPSCR =
     0x00040001 (RM = 01, DN = 1) and the ROM's own FPU self-test at 0x058696 demands the truncated
     answers, so the emulator now computes exactly and truncates.  This trace's script was written and
     verified against the old emulator and stopped at its first float comparison.  What failed, by class:
     * MODEL (Python float arithmetic, i.e. round-to-nearest): the symbolic evaluator's fadd / fsub / fmul /
       fmac / float (ev), the saturating-helper model sat_model, the 0x0BE608 model, and the 0x0BE598 /
       0x0BE588 model (first failure: 0BE598(raw=1, k=0.005, c=-1)).  All now exact + truncated toward
       zero; fmac fused.  None was loosened into a tolerance.
     * HARD-CODED COUNTS from the old emulator: index 0x009, complete window 319 -> 576; first-version grid
       231 -> 338; count-1 boundary "2 .. 128 floats below" -> "1 .. 255"; bound "<= 2^-25" -> "< 2^-24".
       Now derived, not just counted: x' = ceil(x * 2^24) / 2^24 for 0 < x < 0.5, and 2^-24 / ulp(below
       b) - 1 differing inputs per boundary b < 0.5, both asserted exactly.
     * NOT a rounding effect: no script failure.  Every getter formula, all 560 constants, the index-map
       block (391 lines, byte-identical), every setter effect, every dispatch / transport / overrun result
       and every logcfg composite came out unchanged -- shown by replaying the script with round-to-nearest
       patched back in: the two outputs differ only in the index 0x009 line (319 / 231 vs 576 / 338).  The
       script reads neither desc_types nor the census.  But this TEXT quoted the census: the regenerated
       function_census.json now lists dispatch site 0x09A466 and gives 0x09A3AC 151 instructions (both
       re-derived from bytes here: extent() and fn_dump give 151, and the listing of 0x09A466 verifies).
       The census remarks under TABLE GEOMETRY, E2, A1 and H11 are annotated as describing the old census.
     Added checks: every fmac a getter reaches has addend 0 (so fused vs unfused cannot change a getter);
     under truncation sat8(x / 0.0078125) is exactly min(255, floor(x / k + 0.5)) for x >= 0; the pool
     scales 0x3EA0A0A0 / 0x3EC8C8C8 are exactly 80/255 and 100/255 truncated toward zero (E25).
     Contracts: none changed meaning.  The 0x0BE5A8 / 0x0BE5D8 formula clamp(trunc((x - off)/scale + 0.5))
     stands; its steps now carry the chip's rounding, which makes "+ 0.5, ftrc" an exact round-half-up.
     Text repaired: header, G03 class line, 0x05D2C0 MECHANISM / EVIDENCE, 0x0BE5A8 and 0x0BE598 EVIDENCE,
     E24, E25, N8, H3 and the G03 bullet above (marked SUPERSEDED, not rewritten).  No instruction line
     changed; verify_disasm_v2 --rom: 2655 checked / 0 mismatch / 0 skipped, as before.
